Blue band: min-max range across 7 continuation-mode C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: starting from first-generation text ($\\auc_{0v1} \\geq 0.92$), a single additional generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content (1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp; 2v3 falls below $\\theta$); Appendix~\\ref{sec:qualitative} illustrates these depth-dependent changes with representative document chains.\n\n\\paragraph{Operational value of the precise boundary.}\n$\\Kempirical{=}2$ resolves the question of filtering granularity: graduated weighting beyond depth~2 offers no advantage (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}), and the $\\theta$-invariant domain ordering extends this across domains (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}; rate sweeps in Appendix~\\ref{sec:rate_sweep}).\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\\ retrain-chain.}\nAll main results use prompt-chain, so $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects text-intrinsic information loss.\nRetrain-chain experiments yield $\\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, and $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), placing prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as the curation floor and retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ as the upper bound.\n\n\\paragraph{Wikipedia single-generation saturation.}\nWikipedia's template-driven structure causes rapid convergence toward the domain distribution (low TTR and entropy; Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}), eliminating depth signal after a single generation.\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and domain dependence.}\nToken-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}); the ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).\nDomain determines the $\\Kempirical$ ordering; model architecture and scale modulate the absolute level within that ordering.\n\n\\paragraph{Feature-space ceiling and $\\Kempirical_{\\text{15D}}$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical_{\\text{intrinsic}}$.}\n(a)~The domain ordering is \\emph{feature-set invariant}: it holds under both 15D statistics and DeBERTa fine-tuning.\n(b)~Absolute $\\Kempirical$ values are \\emph{feature-dependent}: DeBERTa extends $\\Kempirical$ by $+2$ on Wikipedia and $+1$ on arXiv (Tables~\\ref{tab:wiki_deberta_comparison}, \\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), so 15D provides a conservative lower bound.\n(c)~On C4 and Code, DeBERTa matches the 15D bound ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), establishing a \\emph{representation-independent boundary}; DeBERTa-based filtering offers no advantage over 15D OBD (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_filtering}), confirming $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as operationally binding regardless of feature expressiveness.\nThe progression 15D ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $\\to$ neural probes ($K^*_{\\text{neural}}{=}3$; Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}) $\\to$ DeBERTa ($\\Kempirical{=}4$) shows diminishing returns ($+1$ per upgrade), suggesting $K^*_\\text{theory} \\approx 3$--$4$; permutation importance confirms saturation (all 15 features near-random at 2v3; Appendix~\\ref{sec:feature_importance}).\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-source pooling and transfer.}\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains, partially addressing the 4/6 off-diagonal boundary-level failure at 7B (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}): although $\\Kempirical{=}2$ cannot be assumed when the generator is unknown, domain-calibrated ceilings are preserved under mixed-generator training.\n\n\\paragraph{Mixed-chain scenarios.}\nReal contamination likely involves both processes; our results bound the extremes ($\\Kempirical{=}2$ to $\\Kempirical{=}7$), but whether prompt-chain laundering masks retrain-chain signals remains open.\n\n",

1	\section{Consistency Check: $\Kempirical$-Guided Filtering}
2	\label{sec:filtering}
3	
4	\begin{table}[t]
5	\centering
6	\caption{Downstream evaluation after fine-tuning Qwen-2.5-1.5B on 30k C4 documents (depths 0--5), 3 seeds. Metrics: PIQA (acc\_norm), TruthfulQA (mc2), WinoGrande (acc), ARC-C (acc\_norm), GSM8K (exact match). Bold: best strategy.}
7	\label{tab:tab_filtering_main}
8	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\input{tables/tab_filtering_main}}
9	\end{table}
10	
11	The preceding sections establish $\Kempirical{=}2$ as a robust boundary; we test whether this predicts the correct curation strategy by fine-tuning Qwen-2.5-1.5B on 30{,}000 prompt-chain documents (depths 0--5; Table~\ref{tab:tab_filtering_main}).
12	Graduated filtering performs indistinguishably from no filtering ($\Delta \leq 0.53$\,pp across five benchmarks), confirming that depth weighting beyond $\Kempirical$ captures no signal; binary filtering selectively preserves mathematical reasoning (GSM8K $+$1.92\,pp) and factual accuracy (TruthfulQA $+$1.52\,pp) but degrades commonsense reasoning (WinoGrande $-$0.79\,pp).
13	All strategies incur capability cost relative to the base model; binary filtering partially mitigates this for reasoning tasks.
14	Effect sizes are small (max $+$1.92\,pp), commensurate with the 30k-document training budget; these results demonstrate strategy ordering rather than absolute improvement.
15	Cross-model replication and rate sweeps (10--100\%) confirm graduated $\approx$ no-filter ordering (Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response}, \ref{sec:pythia_filtering}, \ref{sec:rate_sweep}).
16

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01WkKrwUHUTbtZnNZQYNdGik`</summary>

```text
17	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
18	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: starting from first-generation text ($\auc_{0v1} \geq 0.92$), a single additional generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content (1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp; 2v3 falls below $\theta$).
19	
20	\paragraph{Operational value of the precise boundary.}
21	$\Kempirical{=}2$ resolves the question of filtering granularity: graduated weighting beyond depth~2 offers no advantage (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}), and the $\theta$-invariant domain ordering extends this across domains (Table~\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}; rate sweeps in Appendix~\ref{sec:rate_sweep}).
22	
23	\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\ retrain-chain.}
24	All main results use prompt-chain, so $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects text-intrinsic information loss.
25	Retrain-chain experiments yield $\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, and $\Kempirical{\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), placing prompt-chain $\Kempirical{=}2$ as the curation floor and retrain-chain $\Kempirical{=}7$ as the upper bound.
26	
27	\paragraph{Wikipedia single-generation saturation.}
28	Wikipedia's template-driven structure causes rapid convergence toward the domain distribution (low TTR and entropy; Table~\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}), eliminating depth signal after a single generation.
29	
30	\paragraph{JSD decay and domain dependence.}
31	Token-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\times$ slower ($\lambda \approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$; Appendix~\ref{sec:jsd_extended}); the ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).
32	Domain determines the $\Kempirical$ ordering; model architecture and scale modulate the absolute level within that ordering.
33	
34	\paragraph{Feature-space ceiling and $\Kempirical_{\text{15D}}$ vs.\ $\Kempirical_{\text{intrinsic}}$.}
35	(a)~The domain ordering is \emph{feature-set invariant}: it holds under both 15D statistics and DeBERTa fine-tuning.
36	(b)~Absolute $\Kempirical$ values are \emph{feature-dependent}: DeBERTa extends $\Kempirical$ by $+2$ on Wikipedia and $+1$ on arXiv (Tables~\ref{tab:wiki_deberta_comparison}, \ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), so 15D provides a conservative lower bound.
37	(c)~On C4 and Code, DeBERTa matches the 15D bound ($\Kempirical{=}2$), establishing a \emph{representation-independent boundary}; DeBERTa-based filtering offers no advantage over 15D OBD (Table~\ref{tab:deberta_filtering}), confirming $\Kempirical{=}2$ as operationally binding regardless of feature expressiveness.
38	The progression 15D ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $\to$ neural probes ($K^*_{\text{neural}}{=}3$; Section~\ref{sec:neural_probe}) $\to$ DeBERTa ($\Kempirical{=}4$) shows diminishing returns ($+1$ per upgrade), suggesting $K^*_\text{theory} \approx 3$--$4$; permutation importance confirms saturation (all 15 features near-random at 2v3; Appendix~\ref{sec:feature_importance}).
39	
40	\paragraph{Multi-source pooling and transfer.}
41	Multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ across all four domains, partially addressing the 4/6 off-diagonal boundary-level failure at 7B (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}): although $\Kempirical{=}2$ cannot be assumed when the generator is unknown, domain-calibrated ceilings are preserved under mixed-generator training.
42	
43	\paragraph{Mixed-chain scenarios.}
44	Real contamination likely involves both processes; our results bound the extremes ($\Kempirical{=}2$ to $\Kempirical{=}7$), but whether prompt-chain laundering masks retrain-chain signals remains open.
45	
46	
```

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   This is a Paper Agent session for "gen-depth-contamination" targeting EMNLP 2026 ARR submission. The session handles sequential Director patch instructions (Round 24 supplements #2-#5), a full consistency audit, and an urgent page overflow fix. The Paper Agent communicates via `paper_agent_post_to_director` (NOT main_agent_post_to_director). Key constraints: EMNLP 2026 strict 8-page main body limit, no prose dashes except proper nouns.

   **Most recent URGENT request**: Director reports main text spills 4 lines onto page 9. Must compress 4+ lines immediately without deleting substantive content. Candidates: Discussion wording, Section 5 power caveat, Conclusion, Method LOFO.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - K* (K*_empirical): discrimination boundary = max depth where AUC > θ=0.60
   - LOFO: Leave-One-Family-Out cross-validation for θ validation
   - Non-Qwen/Qwen held-out split: derive θ from non-Qwen families, validate on Qwen
   - θ=0.60 positioned as "intentionally conservative" — above LOFO consensus [0.57, 0.59]
   - DeBERTa Wikipedia K* gap: OBD K*=1 → DeBERTa K*=3 (gap = +2, NOT +1)
   - DeBERTa arXiv K* gap: OBD K*=3 → DeBERTa K*=4 (gap = +1)
   - Feature importance: 0v1 driven by Δ¹ Surprisal Std (41.7%), 1v2 by Surprisal Std, 2v3 near-random
   - Permutation importance data from LLaMA-3.1-8B (generator), Qwen-1.5B (scorer)
   - Filtering: graduated ≈ no-filter (Δ≤0.53pp), binary preserves reasoning (GSM8K +1.92pp)

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`docs/paper/discussion.tex`** (MOST CRITICAL for page budget)
     - Multiple edits: DeBERTa +2 fix, tightness+permutation compression, removed qualitative cross-ref
     - Current state (lines 17-45):
     ```latex
     \paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
     For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: starting from first-generation text ($\auc_{0v1} \geq 0.92$), a single additional generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content (1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp; 2v3 falls below $\theta$).
     
     \paragraph{Operational value of the precise boundary.}
     $\Kempirical{=}2$ resolves the question of filtering granularity: graduated weighting beyond depth~2 offers no advantage (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}), and the $\theta$-invariant domain ordering extends this across domains (Table~\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}; rate sweeps in Appendix~\ref{sec:rate_sweep}).
     
     \paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\ retrain-chain.}
     All main results use prompt-chain, so $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects text-intrinsic information loss.
     Retrain-chain experiments yield $\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, and $\Kempirical{\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), placing prompt-chain $\Kempirical{=}2$ as the curation floor and retrain-chain $\Kempirical{=}7$ as the upper bound.
     
     \paragraph{Wikipedia single-generation saturation.}
     Wikipedia's template-driven structure causes rapid convergence toward the domain distribution (low TTR and entropy; Table~\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}), eliminating depth signal after a single generation.
     
     \paragraph{JSD decay and domain dependence.}
     Token-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\times$ slower ($\lambda \approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$; Appendix~\ref{sec:jsd_extended}); the ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).
     Domain determines the $\Kempirical$ ordering; model architecture and scale modulate the absolute level within that ordering.
     
     \paragraph{Feature-space ceiling and $\Kempirical_{\text{15D}}$ vs.\ $\Kempirical_{\text{intrinsic}}$.}
     (a)~The domain ordering is \emph{feature-set invariant}: it holds under both 15D statistics and DeBERTa fine-tuning.
     (b)~Absolute $\Kempirical$ values are \emph{feature-dependent}: DeBERTa extends $\Kempirical$ by $+2$ on Wikipedia and $+1$ on arXiv (Tables~\ref{tab:wiki_deberta_comparison}, \ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), so 15D provides a conservative lower bound.
     (c)~On C4 and Code, DeBERTa matches the 15D bound ($\Kempirical{=}2$), establishing a \emph{representation-independent boundary}; DeBERTa-based filtering offers no advantage over 15D OBD (Table~\ref{tab:deberta_filtering}), confirming $\Kempirical{=}2$ as operationally binding regardless of feature expressiveness.
     The progression 15D ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $\to$ neural probes ($K^*_{\text{neural}}{=}3$; Section~\ref{sec:neural_probe}) $\to$ DeBERTa ($\Kempirical{=}4$) shows diminishing returns ($+1$ per upgrade), suggesting $K^*_\text{theory} \approx 3$--$4$; permutation importance confirms saturation (all 15 features near-random at 2v3; Appendix~\ref{sec:feature_importance}).
     
     \paragraph{Multi-source pooling and transfer.}
     Multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ across all four domains, partially addressing the 4/6 off-diagonal boundary-level failure at 7B (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}): although $\Kempirical{=}2$ cannot be assumed when the generator is unknown, domain-calibrated ceilings are preserved under mixed-generator training.
     
     \paragraph{Mixed-chain scenarios.}
     Real contamination likely involves both processes; our results bound the extremes ($\Kempirical{=}2$ to $\Kempirical{=}7$), but whether prompt-chain laundering masks retrain-chain signals remains open.
     ```

   - **`docs/paper/experiments_c3.tex`** (Section 5, candidate for compression)
     ```latex
     The preceding sections establish $\Kempirical{=}2$ as a robust boundary; we test whether this predicts the correct curation strategy by fine-tuning Qwen-2.5-1.5B on 30{,}000 prompt-chain documents (depths 0--5; Table~\ref{tab:tab_filtering_main}).
     Graduated filtering performs indistinguishably from no filtering ($\Delta \leq 0.53$\,pp across five benchmarks), confirming that depth weighting beyond $\Kempirical$ captures no signal; binary filtering selectively preserves mathematical reasoning (GSM8K $+$1.92\,pp) and factual accuracy (TruthfulQA $+$1.52\,pp) but degrades commonsense reasoning (WinoGrande $-$0.79\,pp).
     All strategies incur capability cost relative to the base model; binary filtering partially mitigates this for reasoning tasks.
     Effect sizes are small (max $+$1.92\,pp), commensurate with the 30k-document training budget; these results demonstrate strategy ordering rather than absolute improvement.
     Cross-model replication and rate sweeps (10--100\%) confirm graduated $\approx$ no-filter ordering (Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response}, \ref{sec:pythia_filtering}, \ref{sec:rate_sweep}).
     ```

   - **`docs/paper/experiments_c1.tex`** (line 98 fixed: +1→+2 on Wikipedia)
     ```latex
     The classifier gap reveals a feature-set-dependent two-tier structure: zero on C4 and Code (DeBERTa $\Kempirical{=}2$, matching OBD), but $+2$ on Wikipedia (DeBERTa $\Kempirical{=}3$; OLMo excluded) and $+1$ on arXiv (DeBERTa $\Kempirical{=}4$, 3v4 $\auc = 0.624$; Table~\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv})
     ```

   - **`docs/paper/appendix_extended.tex`** (multiple additions this session):
     - Lines ~754-787: Non-Qwen/Qwen split table (`\label{tab:nonqwen_split}`)
     - Lines ~1038-1080: Feature Importance Analysis section (`\label{sec:feature_importance}`) with figure and 15-row table
     - Lines ~1085-1089: Qualitative Examples section (`\label{sec:qualitative}`) with 3-sentence intro + `\input{../../artifacts/case_study_qualitative}`

   - **`docs/paper/method.tex`** (lines 30-33, LOFO block - candidate for compression):
     ```latex
     To guard against circularity, we perform leave-one-family-out (LOFO) cross-validation: $\theta$ is derived from five architecture families and evaluated on the held-out sixth.
     Including Gemma-2-2B narrows the consensus range to $\hat{\theta} \in [0.57, 0.59]$; 8/10 held-out predictions match the full-data $\Kempirical$ (mismatches: Qwen-rewrite mode effect; Gemma-2-2B $\Kempirical_{\hat{\theta}}{=}2$ vs.\ $\Kempirical_{0.60}{=}1$).
     As a separate held-out check, deriving $\theta$ from five non-Qwen families (consensus $(0.54, 0.58]$) and validating on three Qwen configurations yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ for all three, confirming that $\theta$ is not overfit to the scorer family.
     $\theta{=}0.60$ sits above both ranges, yielding $\Kempirical$ that are reliable lower bounds (arXiv: 6/6 agreement; Appendix~\ref{sec:theta_lofo}).
     ```

   - **`docs/paper/conclusion.tex`** (4 lines, candidate for compression):
     ```latex
     We present the first empirical measurement of $\Kempirical$ across six architecture families, four domains, and four decoding strategies.
     For web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ holds across four of six families: a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper recursive content.
     The domain ordering arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 $=$ Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$) is feature-set invariant; 15D provides a conservative lower bound.
     For curation, binary filtering suffices ($\Delta \leq 0.53$\,pp for graduated vs.\ no filtering); retrain-chain experiments extend the range to $\Kempirical \in [2, 7]$, with scaling beyond 14B and mixed chains remaining open.
     ```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Cyykezemh3fti8YpUyhimF`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Problem Formulation}
5	\label{sec:formulation}
6	
7	A generation operator $G_\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\delta$) produces successive generations $X \to Y_1 \to \cdots \to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \triangleq X$ (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
8	This sequence forms a Markov chain.
9	By the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \geq I(X; Y_2) \geq \cdots \geq 0$: the original signal degrades monotonically.
10	Together with Markov chain convergence, this implies a finite $\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations become indistinguishable; DPI motivates finite $\Ktheory$ but does not formally guarantee or bound it.
11	Specifically, DPI constrains the cumulative divergence from the seed text; the adjacent-pair convergence that determines $\Kempirical$ follows from the chain's approach to its stationary distribution~\cite{levin2017markov}, where successive transition distributions become indistinguishable.
12	The actual saturation depth must therefore be measured empirically.
13	
14	We define the operational lower bound $K^*_{\text{empirical}}$ (hereafter $\Kempirical$), where $S$ denotes the feature set (specified in Section~\ref{sec:features}):
15	\begin{equation}
16	  \Kempirical = \max\!\bigl\{k \!\geq\! 1 : \mathrm{CI}_{\mathrm{low}}(\auc_{j\text{-}1,j}) > \theta,\ \forall j \!\leq\! k\bigr\},
17	  \label{eq:kstar}
18	\end{equation}
19	where $\mathrm{CI}_{\mathrm{low}}(\auc_{k-1,k})$ is the lower bound of a 95\% bootstrap confidence interval (1{,}000 resamples\footnote{We use 1{,}000 resamples as the default for computational efficiency; arXiv cross-scorer CIs (Table~\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}) use 10{,}000 to achieve tighter bounds at marginal boundaries where sub-pp differences determine the $\Kempirical{=}2$ vs.\ $\Kempirical{=}3$ verdict.}) over the pairwise AUC (Area Under the ROC Curve) between depths $k{-}1$ and $k$, and $\theta = 0.60$ is a post-hoc operational threshold (motivated below).
20	This criterion ensures that $\Kempirical$ reflects statistically robust discriminability; the $\forall j \leq k$ constraint is naturally satisfied because $\auc_{k,k+1}$ decreases monotonically above $\theta$ across all tested configurations (Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci}).
21	While non-monotonic $\auc_{k,k+1}$ is theoretically possible if intermediate generations traverse a high-entropy transient region, we observe strict monotonicity above $\theta$ across all 37 configurations (Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}).
22	$\Kempirical$ is jointly conditioned on the feature set and $\theta$; the domain ordering is stable across $\theta \in [0.575, 0.65]$ (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}). We verify that non-boundary CI upper bounds fall below $\theta$.
23	
24	\paragraph{Threshold choice.}
25	We set $\theta{=}0.60$ at the empirical AUC discontinuity (Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta}): 1v2 values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, creating a ${\sim}$5--11\,pp gap; a 10{,}000-iteration permutation test confirms $p \leq 10^{-4}$ for all boundary comparisons (Benjamini-Hochberg $q_{\max} = 0.003$ across $m{=}30$ tests; Appendix~\ref{sec:bh_correction}).
26	A Youden's J analysis independently validates this choice: the optimal threshold separating boundary from non-boundary AUC values falls at $\theta^*{=}0.605$ ($J{=}1.0$ at config level; $J{=}0.88$ at fold level), placing $\theta{=}0.60$ within the optimal interval $(0.595, 0.611)$~\cite{youden1950index}.
27	We select $\theta$ at this discontinuity because our goal is to identify a natural gap in discriminability rather than to maximize classification performance.
28	$\Kempirical{=}2$ is stable across $\theta \in [0.575, 0.625]$ for all but one marginal configuration (Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}); only at extremes does $\Kempirical$ shift ($\theta{=}0.55$: $\Kempirical{=}3$ for Qwen-rewrite; $\theta{=}0.70$: $\Kempirical{=}1$).
29	The downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}) uses OBD without re-optimizing $\theta$, avoiding circularity.
30	To guard against circularity, we perform leave-one-family-out (LOFO) cross-validation: $\theta$ is derived from five architecture families and evaluated on the held-out sixth.
31	Including Gemma-2-2B narrows the consensus range to $\hat{\theta} \in [0.57, 0.59]$; 8/10 held-out predictions match the full-data $\Kempirical$ (mismatches: Qwen-rewrite mode effect; Gemma-2-2B $\Kempirical_{\hat{\theta}}{=}2$ vs.\ $\Kempirical_{0.60}{=}1$).
32	As a separate held-out check, deriving $\theta$ from five non-Qwen families (consensus $(0.54, 0.58]$) and validating on three Qwen configurations yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ for all three, confirming that $\theta$ is not overfit to the scorer family.
33	$\theta{=}0.60$ sits above both ranges, yielding $\Kempirical$ that are reliable lower bounds (arXiv: 6/6 agreement; Appendix~\ref{sec:theta_lofo}).
34	
35	
36	\subsection{Features and Classifier}
37	\label{sec:features}
38	
39	Each document is characterized by a 15-dimensional feature vector spanning four groups: \emph{surprisal moments} (mean, std, skewness, kurtosis; 4~dims), \emph{surprisal derivatives} (mean and std of first- and second-order differences, plus first-order skewness; 5~dims), \emph{vocabulary diversity} (type-token ratio, hapax legomena ratio, self-BLEU; 3~dims), and \emph{frequency tail statistics} (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction; 3~dims).
40	All features are computed from token-level surprisal values via an external scorer model (Table~\ref{tab:s5_features}).
41	Self-BLEU is computed within each CV fold to prevent leakage; removing it reduces mean $\auc_{1v2}$ by ${<}\,1$\,pp ($\Kempirical$ unchanged; Table~\ref{tab:selfbleu_ablation}).
42	Hand-crafted features enable cross-generator transfer and permit attribution of discriminative power to specific text properties.
43	These features serve as empirically validated proxies for distributional shift across generations, rather than direct measures of mutual information; the monotonic AUC decay observed across all configurations (Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci}) provides post-hoc validation.
44	
45	We use \emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), decomposing the $(K{+}1)$-class problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers, each estimating $P(\text{depth} > k \mid \mathbf{x})$ via LightGBM~\cite{ke2017lightgbm} (hyperparameters in Table~\ref{tab:s6_lgbm_params}); OBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (0.984 vs.\ 1.016) and superior pairwise $\auc$ (0.6095 vs.\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).
46	
47	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
48	\label{sec:experimental_setup}
49	
50	We evaluate ten configurations spanning six architecture families (1B--14B; Table~\ref{tab:setup}), each generating $K{=}5$ depth levels from 5{,}000 C4~\cite{raffel2020t5} seeds (256 tokens; 30{,}000 documents per configuration).
51	Default decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.
52	Continuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.
53	Evaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\auc$ as primary metric.
54	
55	\begin{table}[t]
56	\centering
57	\small
58	\caption{Experimental configurations for depth-chain generation. \textbf{Config}: shorthand identifier; \textbf{Model}: architecture and parameter count; \textbf{Mode}: generation strategy (continuation or rewrite); $N$: number of seed documents per depth level.}
59	\label{tab:setup}
60	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
61	\begin{tabular}{llcc}
62	\toprule
63	\textbf{Config} & \textbf{Model} & \textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\
64	\midrule
65	Qwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\
66	Qwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
67	Qwen-base & Qwen2.5-1.5B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
68	Pythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
69	OLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
70	Gemma     & Gemma-2-2B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
71	Qwen-7B   & Qwen2.5-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
72	Qwen-14B  & Qwen2.5-14B           & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
73	LLaMA-8B  & LLaMA-3.1-8B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
74	Mistral   & Mistral-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
75	\bottomrule
76	\end{tabular}}
77	\end{table}
78

```text
1	\section{Consistency Check: $\Kempirical$-Guided Filtering}
2	\label{sec:filtering}
3	
4	\begin{table}[t]
5	\centering
6	\caption{Downstream evaluation after fine-tuning Qwen-2.5-1.5B on 30k C4 documents (depths 0--5), 3 seeds. Metrics: PIQA (acc\_norm), TruthfulQA (mc2), WinoGrande (acc), ARC-C (acc\_norm), GSM8K (exact match). Bold: best strategy.}
7	\label{tab:tab_filtering_main}
8	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\input{tables/tab_filtering_main}}
9	\end{table}
10	
11	The preceding sections establish $\Kempirical{=}2$ as a robust boundary; we test whether this predicts the correct curation strategy by fine-tuning Qwen-2.5-1.5B on 30{,}000 prompt-chain documents (depths 0--5; Table~\ref{tab:tab_filtering_main}).
12	Graduated filtering performs indistinguishably from no filtering ($\Delta \leq 0.53$\,pp across five benchmarks), confirming that depth weighting beyond $\Kempirical$ captures no signal; binary filtering selectively preserves mathematical reasoning (GSM8K $+$1.92\,pp) and factual accuracy (TruthfulQA $+$1.52\,pp) but degrades commonsense reasoning (WinoGrande $-$0.79\,pp).
13	All strategies incur capability cost relative to the base model; binary filtering partially mitigates this for reasoning tasks.
14	Effect sizes are small (max $+$1.92\,pp), commensurate with the 30k-document training budget; these results demonstrate strategy ordering rather than absolute improvement.
15	Cross-model replication and rate sweeps (10--100\%) confirm graduated $\approx$ no-filter ordering (Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response}, \ref{sec:pythia_filtering}, \ref{sec:rate_sweep}).
16	
```

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	We present the first empirical measurement of $\Kempirical$ across six architecture families, four domains, and four decoding strategies.
5	For web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ holds across four of six families: a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper recursive content.
6	The domain ordering arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 $=$ Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$) is feature-set invariant; 15D provides a conservative lower bound.
7	For curation, binary filtering suffices ($\Delta \leq 0.53$\,pp for graduated vs.\ no filtering); retrain-chain experiments extend the range to $\Kempirical \in [2, 7]$, with scaling beyond 14B and mixed chains remaining open.
8

    "newString": "\\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\\ retrain-chain.}\nAll main results use prompt-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}2$, text-intrinsic information loss).\nRetrain-chain yields $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), placing prompt-chain as the curation floor and retrain-chain as the upper bound.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}\n\\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 7 continuation-mode C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: starting from first-generation text ($\\auc_{0v1} \\geq 0.92$), a single additional generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content (1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp; 2v3 falls below $\\theta$).\n\n\\paragraph{Operational value of the precise boundary.}\n$\\Kempirical{=}2$ resolves the question of filtering granularity: graduated weighting beyond depth~2 offers no advantage (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}), and the $\\theta$-invariant domain ordering extends this across domains (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}; rate sweeps in Appendix~\\ref{sec:rate_sweep}).\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\\ retrain-chain.}\nAll main results use prompt-chain, so $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects text-intrinsic information loss.\nRetrain-chain experiments yield $\\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, and $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), placing prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as the curation floor and retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ as the upper bound.\n\n\\paragraph{Wikipedia single-generation saturation.}\nWikipedia's template-driven structure causes rapid convergence toward the domain distribution (low TTR and entropy; Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}), eliminating depth signal after a single generation.\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and domain dependence.}\nToken-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}); the ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).\nDomain determines the $\\Kempirical$ ordering; model architecture and scale modulate the absolute level within that ordering.\n\n\\paragraph{Feature-space ceiling and $\\Kempirical_{\\text{15D}}$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical_{\\text{intrinsic}}$.}\n(a)~The domain ordering is \\emph{feature-set invariant}: it holds under both 15D statistics and DeBERTa fine-tuning.\n(b)~Absolute $\\Kempirical$ values are \\emph{feature-dependent}: DeBERTa extends $\\Kempirical$ by $+2$ on Wikipedia and $+1$ on arXiv (Tables~\\ref{tab:wiki_deberta_comparison}, \\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), so 15D provides a conservative lower bound.\n(c)~On C4 and Code, DeBERTa matches the 15D bound ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), establishing a \\emph{representation-independent boundary}; DeBERTa-based filtering offers no advantage over 15D OBD (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_filtering}), confirming $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as operationally binding regardless of feature expressiveness.\nThe progression 15D ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $\\to$ neural probes ($K^*_{\\text{neural}}{=}3$; Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}) $\\to$ DeBERTa ($\\Kempirical{=}4$) shows diminishing returns ($+1$ per upgrade), suggesting $K^*_\\text{theory} \\approx 3$--$4$; permutation importance confirms saturation (all 15 features near-random at 2v3; Appendix~\\ref{sec:feature_importance}).\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-source pooling and transfer.}\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains, partially addressing the 4/6 off-diagonal boundary-level failure at 7B (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}): although $\\Kempirical{=}2$ cannot be assumed when the generator is unknown, domain-calibrated ceilings are preserved under mixed-generator training.\n\n\\paragraph{Mixed-chain scenarios.}\nReal contamination likely involves both processes; our results bound the extremes ($\\Kempirical{=}2$ to $\\Kempirical{=}7$), but whether prompt-chain laundering masks retrain-chain signals remains open.\n\n",

C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: starting from first-generation text ($\\auc_{0v1} \\geq 0.92$), a single additional generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content (1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp; rate sweeps in Appendix~\\ref{sec:rate_sweep}).\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\\ retrain-chain.}\nAll main results use prompt-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}2$, text-intrinsic information loss).\nRetrain-chain yields $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), placing prompt-chain as the curation floor and retrain-chain as the upper bound.\n\n\\paragraph{Wikipedia single-generation saturation.}\nWikipedia's template-driven structure causes rapid convergence toward the domain distribution (low TTR and entropy; Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}), eliminating depth signal after a single generation.\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and domain dependence.}\nToken-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}); the ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).\nDomain determines the $\\Kempirical$ ordering; model architecture and scale modulate the absolute level within that ordering.\n\n\\paragraph{Feature-space ceiling and $\\Kempirical_{\\text{15D}}$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical_{\\text{intrinsic}}$.}\n(a)~The domain ordering is \\emph{feature-set invariant}: it holds under both 15D statistics and DeBERTa fine-tuning.\n(b)~Absolute $\\Kempirical$ values are \\emph{feature-dependent}: DeBERTa extends $\\Kempirical$ by $+2$ on Wikipedia and $+1$ on arXiv (Tables~\\ref{tab:wiki_deberta_comparison}, \\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), so 15D provides a conservative lower bound.\n(c)~On C4 and Code, DeBERTa matches the 15D bound ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), establishing a \\emph{representation-independent boundary}; DeBERTa-based filtering offers no advantage over 15D OBD (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_filtering}), confirming $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as operationally binding regardless of feature expressiveness.\nThe progression 15D ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $\\to$ neural probes ($K^*_{\\text{neural}}{=}3$; Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}) $\\to$ DeBERTa ($\\Kempirical{=}4$) shows diminishing returns ($+1$ per upgrade), suggesting $K^*_\\text{theory} \\approx 3$--$4$; permutation importance confirms saturation (all 15 features near-random at 2v3; Appendix~\\ref{sec:feature_importance}).\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-source pooling and transfer.}\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains, partially addressing the 4/6 off-diagonal boundary-level failure at 7B (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}): although $\\Kempirical{=}2$ cannot be assumed when the generator is unknown, domain-calibrated ceilings are preserved under mixed-generator training.\n\n\\paragraph{Mixed-chain scenarios.}\nReal contamination likely involves both processes;     "oldString": "Including Gemma-2-2B narrows the consensus range to $\\hat{\\theta} \\in [0.57, 0.59]$; 8/10 held-out predictions match the full-data $\\Kempirical$ (mismatches: Qwen-rewrite mode effect; Gemma-2-2B $\\Kempirical_{\\hat{\\theta}}{=}2$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical_{0.60}{=}1$).\nAs a separate held-out check, deriving $\\theta$ from five non-Qwen families (consensus $(0.54, 0.58]$) and validating on three Qwen configurations yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for all three, confirming that $\\theta$ is not overfit to the scorer family.\n$\\theta{=}0.60$ sits above both ranges, yielding $\\Kempirical$ that are reliable lower bounds (arXiv: 6/6 agreement; Appendix~\\ref{sec:theta_lofo}).",
    "newString": "Including Gemma-2-2B narrows the consensus range to $\\hat{\\theta} \\in [0.57, 0.59]$; 8/10 held-out predictions match the full-data $\\Kempirical$ (mismatches: Qwen-rewrite mode effect; Gemma-2-2B $\\Kempirical_{\\hat{\\theta}}{=}2$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical_{0.60}{=}1$).\nA separate non-Qwen held-out check (consensus $(0.54, 0.58]$, validated on three Qwen configs) confirms $\\theta$ is not overfit to the scorer family.\n$\\theta{=}0.60$ sits above both ranges, yielding reliable lower bounds (Appendix~\\ref{sec:theta_lofo}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\n\\label{sec:formulation}\n\nA generation operator $G_\\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\\delta$) produces successive generations $X \\to Y_1 \\to \\cdots \\to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \\triangleq X$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nThis sequence forms a Markov chain.\nBy the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \\geq I(X; Y_2) \\geq \\cdots \\geq 0$: the original signal degrades monotonically.\nTogether with Markov chain convergence, this implies a finite $\\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations become indistinguishable; DPI motivates finite $\\Ktheory$ but does not formally guarantee or bound it.\nSpecifically, DPI constrains the cumulative divergence from the seed text; the adjacent-pair convergence that determines $\\Kempirical$ follows from the chain's approach to its stationary distribution~\\cite{levin2017markov}, where successive transition distributions become indistinguishable.\nThe actual saturation depth must therefore be measured empirically.\n\nWe define the operational lower bound $K^*_{\\text{empirical}}$ (hereafter $\\Kempirical$), where $S$ denotes the feature set (specified in Section~\\ref{sec:features}):\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\Kempirical = \\max\\!\\bigl\\{k \\!\\geq\\! 1 : \\mathrm{CI}_{\\mathrm{low}}(\\auc_{j\\text{-}1,j}) > \\theta,\\ \\forall j \\!\\leq\\! k\\bigr\\},\n  \\label{eq:kstar}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathrm{CI}_{\\mathrm{low}}(\\auc_{k-1,k})$ is the lower bound of a 95\\% bootstrap confidence interval (1{,}000 resamples\\footnote{We use 1{,}000 resamples as the default for computational efficiency; arXiv cross-scorer CIs (Table~\\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}) use 10{,}000 to achieve tighter bounds at marginal boundaries where sub-pp differences determine the $\\Kempirical{=}2$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical{=}3$ verdict.}) over the pairwise AUC (Area Under the ROC Curve) between depths $k{-}1$ and $k$, and $\\theta = 0.60$ is a post-hoc operational threshold (motivated below).\nThis criterion ensures that $\\Kempirical$ reflects statistically robust discriminability; the $\\forall j \\leq k$ constraint is naturally satisfied because $\\auc_{k,k+1}$ decreases monotonically above $\\theta$ across all tested configurations (Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci}).\nWhile non-monotonic $\\auc_{k,k+1}$ is theoretically possible if intermediate generations traverse a high-entropy transient region, we observe strict monotonicity above $\\theta$ across all 37 configurations (Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}).\n$\\Kempirical$ is jointly conditioned on the feature set and $\\theta$; the domain ordering is stable across $\\theta \\in [0.575, 0.65]$ (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}). We verify that non-boundary CI upper bounds fall below $\\theta$.\n\n\\paragraph{Threshold choice.}\nWe set $\\theta{=}0.60$ at the empirical AUC discontinuity (Figure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta}): 1v2 values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, creating a ${\\sim}$5--11\\,pp gap; a 10{,}000-iteration permutation test confirms $p \\leq 10^{-4}$ for all boundary comparisons (Benjamini-Hochberg $q_{\\max} = 0.003$ across $m{=}30$ tests; Appendix~\\ref{sec:bh_correction}).\nA Youden's J analysis independently validates this choice: the optimal threshold separating boundary from non-boundary AUC values falls at $\\theta^*{=}0.605$ ($J{=}1.0$ at config level; $J{=}0.88$ at fold level), placing $\\theta{=}0.60$ within the optimal interval $(0.595, 0.611)$~\\cite{youden1950index}.\nWe select $\\theta$ at this discontinuity because our goal is to identify a natural gap in discriminability rather than to maximize classification performance.\n$\\Kempirical{=}2$ is stable across $\\theta \\in [0.575, 0.625]$ for all but one marginal configuration (Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}); only at extremes does $\\Kempirical$ shift ($\\theta{=}0.55$: $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for Qwen-rewrite; $\\theta{=}0.70$: $\\Kempirical{=}1$).\nThe downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) uses OBD without re-optimizing $\\theta$, avoiding circularity.\nTo guard against circularity, we perform leave-one-family-out (LOFO) cross-validation: $\\theta$ is derived from five architecture families and evaluated on the held-out sixth.\nIncluding Gemma-2-2B narrows the consensus range to $\\hat{\\theta} \\in [0.57, 0.59]$; 8/10 held-out predictions match the full-data $\\Kempirical$ (mismatches: Qwen-rewrite mode effect; Gemma-2-2B $\\Kempirical_{\\hat{\\theta}}{=}2$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical_{0.60}{=}1$).\nAs a separate held-out check, deriving $\\theta$ from five non-Qwen families (consensus $(0.54, 0.58]$) and validating on three Qwen configurations yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for all three, confirming that $\\theta$ is not overfit to the scorer family.\n$\\theta{=}0.60$ sits above both ranges, yielding $\\Kempirical$ that are reliable lower bounds (arXiv: 6/6 agreement; Appendix~\\ref{sec:theta_lofo}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Features and Classifier}\n\\label{sec:features}\n\nEach document is characterized by a 15-dimensional feature vector spanning four groups: \\emph{surprisal moments} (mean, std, skewness, kurtosis; 4~dims), \\emph{surprisal derivatives} (mean and std of first- and second-order differences, plus first-order skewness; 5~dims), \\emph{vocabulary diversity} (type-token ratio, hapax legomena ratio, self-BLEU; 3~dims), and \\emph{frequency tail statistics} (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction; 3~dims).\nAll features are computed from token-level surprisal values via an external scorer model (Table~\\ref{tab:s5_features}).\nSelf-BLEU is computed within each CV fold to prevent leakage; removing it reduces mean $\\auc_{1v2}$ by ${<}\\,1$\\,pp ($\\Kempirical$ unchanged; Table~\\ref{tab:selfbleu_ablation}).\nHand-crafted features enable cross-generator transfer and permit attribution of discriminative power to specific text properties.\nThese features serve as empirically validated proxies for distributional shift across generations, rather than direct measures of mutual information; the monotonic AUC decay observed across all configurations (Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci}) provides post-hoc validation.\n\nWe use \\emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), decomposing the $(K{+}1)$-class problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers, each estimating $P(\\text{depth} > k \\mid \\mathbf{x})$ via LightGBM~\\cite{ke2017lightgbm} (hyperparameters in Table~\\ref{tab:s6_lgbm_params}); OBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (0.984 vs.\\ 1.016) and superior pairwise $\\auc$ (0.6095 vs.\\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:experimental_setup}\n\nWe evaluate ten configurations spanning six architecture families (1B--14B; Table~\\ref{tab:setup}), each generating $K{=}5$ depth levels from 5{,}000 C4~\\cite{raffel2020t5} seeds (256 tokens; 30{,}000 documents per configuration).\nDefault decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.\nContinuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.\nEvaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\\auc$ as primary metric.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Experimental configurations for depth-chain generation. \\textbf{Config}: shorthand identifier; \\textbf{Model}: architecture and parameter count; \\textbf{Mode}: generation strategy (continuation or rewrite); $N$: number of seed documents per depth level.}\n\\label{tab:setup}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{llcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Config} & \\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-base & Qwen2.5-1.5B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nPythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nOLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nGemma     & Gemma-2-2B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-7B   & Qwen2.5-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-14B  & Qwen2.5-14B           & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nLLaMA-8B  & LLaMA-3.1-8B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nMistral   & Mistral-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n",

Dashed line: $\theta {=}0.60$; extrapolated 7v8 $\approx 0.57$ (below $\theta $), suggesting $\Kempirical {=}7$ is near the ceiling}{figure.caption.77}{}}

0	% EMNLP 2026 Main - ARR May 2026
1	% Generational Depth Contamination
2	\documentclass[11pt]{article}

21	\newcommand{\Kstar}{{K^*}}
22	\newcommand{\Kempirical}{{K^*}}
23	\newcommand{\Ktheory}{{K^*_{\text{theory}}}}
24	\newcommand{\auc}{\text{AUC}}
25	\newcommand{\jsd}{\text{JSD}}
26	\newcommand{\tpm}{\ensuremath{\,\pm\,}}
27	
28	\title{Generational Depth Estimation of Recursive Synthetic Text:\\Measuring the Discrimination Boundary}
29	
30	\author{Anonymous}
31	
32	\begin{document}
33	\maketitle
34	
35	% === Abstract ===
36	\begin{abstract}
37	Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.
38	We measure the discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$, defined as the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain statistically distinguishable, under prompt-chain generation using largely generator-invariant statistical features and an ordinal classifier.
39	The $\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across six architecture families ($\leq$14B); within this hierarchy, $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ for web text emerges as the dominant mode (four of six families; changepoint-confirmed).
40	The domain ordering is feature-set invariant (preserved under both 15D statistics and DeBERTa fine-tuning), while absolute $\Kempirical$ values are feature-dependent: DeBERTa matches the 15D bound on C4 and Code but extends $\Kempirical$ to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv, placing 15D measurements as conservative lower bounds.
41	Multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ across all four domains with negligible generator-identity signal; features transfer across generators (80--92\% aggregate accuracy, driven by high 0v1 separability), but boundary-level $\Kempirical$ assignments fail under cross-model transfer (4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\theta$).
42	As a consistency check, $\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives for web text.
43	\end{abstract}
44	
45	% === Section 1: Introduction ===
46	\input{introduction}
47	
48	% === Section 2: Related Work ===
49	\input{related_work}
50	
51	% === Section 3: Method ===
52	\input{method}
53	
54	% === Section 4: Experiments ===
55	\input{experiments_c1}
56	
57	% === Section 5: K*-Aware Data Filtering ===
58	\input{experiments_c3}
59	
60	% === Section 6: Discussion ===
61	\input{discussion}
62	
63	% === Section 7: Conclusion ===
64	\input{conclusion}
65	
66	% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===
67	\section*{Limitations}
68	
69	Our findings are bounded by:
70	(1)~\emph{Scope}: full 10-configuration characterization at $\leq$14B (6 architecture families; Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B competence-limited at $\Kempirical{=}1$), with 32B-scale validation via LightGBM and DeBERTa (Section~\ref{sec:neural_probe}); English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \theta$); our Code domain uses CodeSearchNet (2019), which may not fully represent current LLM-generated code patterns; subsampling analysis indicates $n{\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\% reliable $\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\ref{sec:power_curve}).
71	The 1.5B$\to$32B trend shows stable $\Kempirical{=}2$ with declining 1v2 AUC; whether this holds at 70B+ scales remains unverified.
72	(2)~\emph{Protocol}: all main experiments use prompt-chain, which isolates text-intrinsic degradation from training dynamics; real-world contamination involves both prompting and retraining, and retrain-chain yields $\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, $\Kempirical{\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), so prompt-chain $\Kempirical$ may underestimate discriminability in mixed settings.
73	Mixed prompt-chain and retrain-chain scenarios, which better approximate real contamination pathways, are not addressed; preliminary bounds are discussed in Section~\ref{sec:discussion}.
74	We focus on continuation mode as a controlled setting; instruction-tuned models introduce system prompts and RLHF-induced stylistic shifts that confound depth estimation.
75	(3)~\emph{Threshold}: $\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\theta{=}0.70$; 7/9 continuation-mode configurations yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ (Gemma-2-2B, Mistral-7B: $\Kempirical{=}1$); a $\theta$-free changepoint analysis agrees within $\pm 1$ for 35/37 configurations (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
76	(4)~\emph{Marginal boundary}: Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\theta$ by only 0.002; cross-scorer validation drops this to $\Kempirical{=}1$ (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}). LLaMA-3.1-8B (C4) and Mistral-7B-v0.3 (code) represent the most marginal $\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, with 1v2 CI lower bounds within 0.3 percentage points of $\theta$.
77	(5)~\emph{Feature set}: the 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher); $K^*_{\text{neural}}$ is model-dependent ($3$ for Qwen, $2$ for Pythia/OLMo).
78	(6)~\emph{Segment length}: $\Kempirical{=}2$ confirmed for 128 and 256 tokens (CI lower $> \theta$); 512 tokens yields point estimate $\Kempirical{=}2$ but CI lower marginally below $\theta$ ($0.5979$); the boundary weakens at longer generation lengths.
79	(7)~\emph{Curation applicability}: the filtering evaluation is validated on two architectures (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct and Pythia-1.4B) with consistent strategy ordering (Appendix~\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}), though absolute improvement remains small and effect sizes diminish at lower contamination ratios (10--50\%; Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response}).
80	(8)~\emph{Family balance}: the Qwen2.5 family provides 5 of 10 total configurations (4 of 9 continuation-mode); though spanning three scales (1.5B, 7B, 14B) and two training variants (base, instruct), aggregate statistics weight this family more heavily than the five single-configuration families.
81	The pipeline scales linearly with corpus size; the computational bottleneck is scorer inference, which can be parallelized across documents.
82	(9)~\emph{Transfer coverage}: cross-model transfer validation shows 80--92\% accuracy retention at $\leq$1.5B (4 configurations) and 96--100\% mean pairwise AUC retention at 7B (3 models, 3 architecture families; Appendix~\ref{sec:7b_transfer}); however, at the $\Kempirical$ boundary (1v2), 4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\theta$, indicating that boundary-level transfer remains fragile even as aggregate transfer strengthens with scale.
83	(10)~\emph{Mixed chains}: estimating $\Kempirical$ under interleaved prompt-chain and retrain-chain contamination (the dominant real-world scenario) requires chain-type annotation not available in web corpora; our $[2,7]$ bounds may not compose additively.
84	(11)~\emph{Feature ceiling}: larger or domain-adapted encoder scorers may tighten the $\Kempirical$ upper bound; our 15D measurement uses a fixed Pythia-1.4B scorer, and DeBERTa-large extends $\Kempirical$ by $+1$ only on structured domains, suggesting headroom remains.
85	Additional limitations (cross-model scoring sensitivity, feature ranking variability at the 1v2 boundary, structural composition confound analysis) appear in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.
86	
87	% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===
88	\section*{Ethics Statement}
89	
90	Generational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing, positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.
91	Our experiments use publicly available corpora (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv abstracts) and do not involve personally identifiable information.
92	A potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\auc \geq 0.96$ at 0v1) remains robust.
93	Code and generated corpora will be released upon acceptance to enable reproducibility.
94	
95	\bibliography{references}
96	
97	\input{appendix}
98	\input{appendix_extended}

Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B competence-limited at $\\Kempirical{=}1$), with 32B-scale validation via LightGBM and DeBERTa (Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}); English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\\Kempirical{=}2$; ```text
66	
67	% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===
68	\section*{Limitations}
69	
70	Our findings are bounded by:
71	(1)~\emph{Scope}: full 10-configuration characterization at $\leq$14B (6 architecture families; Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B competence-limited at $\Kempirical{=}1$), with 32B-scale validation via LightGBM and DeBERTa (Section~\ref{sec:neural_probe}); English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \theta$); our Code domain uses CodeSearchNet (2019), which may not fully represent current LLM-generated code patterns; subsampling analysis indicates $n{\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\% reliable $\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\ref{sec:power_curve}).
72	The 1.5B$\to$32B trend shows stable $\Kempirical{=}2$ with declining 1v2 AUC; whether this holds at 70B+ scales remains unverified.
73	(2)~\emph{Protocol}: all main experiments use prompt-chain, which isolates text-intrinsic degradation from training dynamics; real-world contamination involves both prompting and retraining, and retrain-chain yields $\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, $\Kempirical{\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), so prompt-chain $\Kempirical$ may underestimate discriminability in mixed settings.
74	Mixed prompt-chain and retrain-chain scenarios, which better approximate real contamination pathways, are not addressed; preliminary bounds are discussed in Section~\ref{sec:discussion}.
75	We focus on continuation mode as a controlled setting; instruction-tuned models introduce system prompts and RLHF-induced stylistic shifts that confound depth estimation.
76	(3)~\emph{Threshold}: $\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\theta{=}0.70$; 7/9 continuation-mode configurations yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ (Gemma-2-2B, Mistral-7B: $\Kempirical{=}1$); a $\theta$-free changepoint analysis agrees within $\pm 1$ for 35/37 configurations (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
77	(4)~\emph{Marginal boundary}: Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\theta$ by only 0.002; cross-scorer validation drops this to $\Kempirical{=}1$ (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}). LLaMA-3.1-8B (C4) and Mistral-7B-v0.3 (code) represent the most marginal $\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, with 1v2 CI lower bounds within 0.3 percentage points of $\theta$.
78	(5)~\emph{Feature set}: the 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher); $K^*_{\text{neural}}$ is model-dependent ($3$ for Qwen, $2$ for Pythia/OLMo).
79	(6)~\emph{Segment length}: $\Kempirical{=}2$ confirmed for 128 and 256 tokens (CI lower $> \theta$); 512 tokens yields point estimate $\Kempirical{=}2$ but CI lower marginally below $\theta$ ($0.5979$); the boundary weakens at longer generation lengths.
80	(7)~\emph{Curation applicability}: the filtering evaluation is validated on two architectures (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct and Pythia-1.4B) with consistent strategy ordering (Appendix~\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}), though absolute improvement remains small and effect sizes diminish at lower contamination ratios (10--50\%; Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response}).
81	(8)~\emph{Family balance}: the Qwen2.5 family provides 5 of 10 total configurations (4 of 9 continuation-mode); though spanning three scales (1.5B, 7B, 14B) and two training variants (base, instruct), aggregate statistics weight this family more heavily than the five single-configuration families.
82	The pipeline scales linearly with corpus size; the computational bottleneck is scorer inference, which can be parallelized across documents.
83	(9)~\emph{Transfer coverage}: cross-model transfer validation shows 80--92\% accuracy retention at $\leq$1.5B (4 configurations) and 96--100\% mean pairwise AUC retention at 7B (3 models, 3 architecture families; Appendix~\ref{sec:7b_transfer}); however, at the $\Kempirical$ boundary (1v2), 4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\theta$, indicating that boundary-level transfer remains fragile even as aggregate transfer strengthens with scale.
84	(10)~\emph{Mixed chains}: estimating $\Kempirical$ under interleaved prompt-chain and retrain-chain contamination (the dominant real-world scenario) requires chain-type annotation not available in web corpora; our $[2,7]$ bounds may not compose additively.
85	(11)~\emph{Feature ceiling}: larger or domain-adapted encoder scorers may tighten the $\Kempirical$ upper bound; our 15D measurement uses a fixed Pythia-1.4B scorer, and DeBERTa-large extends $\Kempirical$ by $+1$ only on structured domains, suggesting headroom remains.
86	Additional limitations (cross-model scoring sensitivity, feature ranking variability at the 1v2 boundary, structural composition confound analysis) appear in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.
87	
88	% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===
89	\section*{Ethics Statement}
90	
```

    "oldString": "Our findings are bounded by:\n(1)~\\emph{Scope}: full 10-configuration characterization at $\\leq$14B (6 architecture families; Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B competence-limited at $\\Kempirical{=}1$), with 32B-scale validation via LightGBM and DeBERTa (Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}); English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \\theta$); our Code domain uses CodeSearchNet (2019), which may not fully represent current LLM-generated code patterns; subsampling analysis indicates $n{\\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\\% reliable $\\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\\ref{sec:power_curve}).\nThe 1.5B$\\to$32B trend shows stable $\\Kempirical{=}2$ with declining 1v2 AUC; whether this holds at 70B+ scales remains unverified.\n(2)~\\emph{Protocol}: all main experiments use prompt-chain, which isolates text-intrinsic degradation from training dynamics; real-world contamination involves both prompting and retraining, and retrain-chain yields $\\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), so prompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ may underestimate discriminability in mixed settings.\nMixed prompt-chain and retrain-chain scenarios, which better approximate real contamination pathways, are not addressed; preliminary bounds are discussed in Section~\\ref{sec:discussion}.\nWe focus on continuation mode as a controlled setting; instruction-tuned models introduce system prompts and RLHF-induced stylistic shifts that confound depth estimation.\n(3)~\\emph{Threshold}: $\\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$; 7/9 continuation-mode configurations yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Gemma-2-2B, Mistral-7B: $\\Kempirical{=}1$); a $\\theta$-free changepoint analysis agrees within $\\pm 1$ for 35/37 configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n(4)~\\emph{Marginal boundary}: Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\\theta$ by only 0.002; cross-scorer validation drops this to $\\Kempirical{=}1$ (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}). LLaMA-3.1-8B (C4) and Mistral-7B-v0.3 (code) represent the most marginal $\\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, with 1v2 CI lower bounds within 0.3 percentage points of $\\theta$.\n(5)~\\emph{Feature set}: the 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher); $K^*_{\\text{neural}}$ is model-dependent ($3$ for Qwen, $2$ for Pythia/OLMo).\n(6)~\\emph{Segment length}: $\\Kempirical{=}2$ confirmed for 128 and 256 tokens (CI lower $> \\theta$); 512 tokens yields point estimate $\\Kempirical{=}2$ but CI lower marginally below $\\theta$ ($0.5979$); the boundary weakens at longer generation lengths.\n(7)~\\emph{Curation applicability}: the filtering evaluation is validated on two architectures (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct and Pythia-1.4B) with consistent strategy ordering (Appendix~\\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}), though absolute improvement remains small and effect sizes diminish at lower contamination ratios (10--50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).\n(8)~\\emph{Family balance}: the Qwen2.5 family provides 5 of 10 total configurations (4 of 9 continuation-mode); though spanning three scales (1.5B, 7B, 14B) and two training variants (base, instruct), aggregate statistics weight this family more heavily than the five single-configuration families.\nThe pipeline scales linearly with corpus size; the computational bottleneck is scorer inference, which can be parallelized across documents.\n(9)~\\emph{Transfer coverage}: cross-model transfer validation shows 80--92\\% accuracy retention at $\\leq$1.5B (4 configurations) and 96--100\\% mean pairwise AUC retention at 7B (3 models, 3 architecture families; Appendix~\\ref{sec:7b_transfer}); however, at the $\\Kempirical$ boundary (1v2), 4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$, indicating that boundary-level transfer remains fragile even as aggregate transfer strengthens with scale.\n(10)~\\emph{Mixed chains}: estimating $\\Kempirical$ under interleaved prompt-chain and retrain-chain contamination (the dominant real-world scenario) requires chain-type annotation not available in web corpora; our $[2,7]$ bounds may not compose additively.\n(11)~\\emph{Feature ceiling}: larger or domain-adapted encoder scorers may tighten the $\\Kempirical$ upper bound; our 15D measurement uses a fixed Pythia-1.4B scorer, and DeBERTa-large extends $\\Kempirical$ by $+1$ only on structured domains, suggesting headroom remains.\nAdditional limitations (cross-model scoring sensitivity, feature ranking variability at the 1v2 boundary, structural composition confound analysis) appear in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.",
    "newString": "All experiments are conducted at $\\leq$14B scale (6 architecture families, 10 configurations) with English text across four domains and four decoding strategies; whether $\\Kempirical{=}2$ holds at 70B+ scales remains unverified.\nMain results use prompt-chain only, isolating text-intrinsic degradation; retrain-chain yields $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), and mixed-chain scenarios are not addressed.\nCross-model transfer retains 80--92\\% aggregate accuracy but boundary-level $\\Kempirical$ assignments remain fragile (4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$; Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\nThe 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound; DeBERTa extends $\\Kempirical$ by $+1$--$2$ on structured domains, suggesting headroom remains.\n$\\Kempirical$ is $\\theta$-dependent (ranges from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$), though a $\\theta$-free changepoint analysis agrees within $\\pm 1$ for 35/37 configurations.\nThe filtering evaluation validates on two architectures with small effect sizes (max $+$1.92\\,pp), demonstrating strategy ordering rather than large absolute gains.\nExtended limitations appear in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.",
    "originalFile": "% EMNLP 2026 Main - ARR May 2026\n% Generational Depth Contamination\n\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}\n\\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}\n\\usepackage{microtype}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{enumitem}\n\\usepackage{subcaption}\n\\usepackage{pifont}\n\\newcommand{\\cmark}{\\ding{51}}\n\\newcommand{\\xmark}{\\ding{55}}\n\\usepackage{placeins}\n\n\\newcommand{\\Kstar}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Kempirical}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Ktheory}{{K^*_{\\text{theory}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\auc}{\\text{AUC}}\n\\newcommand{\\jsd}{\\text{JSD}}\n\\newcommand{\\tpm}{\\ensuremath{\\,\\pm\\,}}\n\n\\title{Generational Depth Estimation of Recursive Synthetic Text:\\\\Measuring the Discrimination Boundary}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n% === Abstract ===\n\\begin{abstract}\nWeb-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.\nWe measure the discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$, defined as the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain statistically distinguishable, under prompt-chain generation using largely generator-invariant statistical features and an ordinal classifier.\nThe $\\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across six architecture families ($\\leq$14B); within this hierarchy, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ for web text emerges as the dominant mode (four of six families; changepoint-confirmed).\nThe domain ordering is feature-set invariant (preserved under both 15D statistics and DeBERTa fine-tuning), while absolute $\\Kempirical$ values are feature-dependent: DeBERTa matches the 15D bound on C4 and Code but extends $\\Kempirical$ to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv, placing 15D measurements as conservative lower bounds.\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains with negligible generator-identity signal; features transfer across generators (80--92\\% aggregate accuracy, driven by high 0v1 separability), but boundary-level $\\Kempirical$ assignments fail under cross-model transfer (4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$).\nAs a consistency check, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives for web text.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n% === Section 1: Introduction ===\n\\input{introduction}\n\n% === Section 2: Related Work ===\n\\input{related_work}\n\n% === Section 3: Method ===\n\\input{method}\n\n% === Section 4: Experiments ===\n\\input{experiments_c1}\n\n% === Section 5: K*-Aware Data Filtering ===\n\\input{experiments_c3}\n\n% === Section 6: Discussion ===\n\\input{discussion}\n\n% === Section 7: Conclusion ===\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur findings are bounded by:\n(1)~\\emph{Scope}: full 10-configuration characterization at $\\leq$14B (6 architecture families; Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B competence-limited at $\\Kempirical{=}1$), with 32B-scale validation via LightGBM and DeBERTa (Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}); English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \\theta$); our Code domain uses CodeSearchNet (2019), which may not fully represent current LLM-generated code patterns; subsampling analysis indicates $n{\\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\\% reliable $\\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\\ref{sec:power_curve}).\nThe 1.5B$\\to$32B trend shows stable $\\Kempirical{=}2$ with declining 1v2 AUC; whether this holds at 70B+ scales remains unverified.\n(2)~\\emph{Protocol}: all main experiments use prompt-chain, which isolates text-intrinsic degradation from training dynamics; real-world contamination involves both prompting and retraining, and retrain-chain yields $\\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), so prompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ may underestimate discriminability in mixed settings.\nMixed prompt-chain and retrain-chain scenarios, which better approximate real contamination pathways, are not addressed; preliminary bounds are discussed in Section~\\ref{sec:discussion}.\nWe focus on continuation mode as a controlled setting; instruction-tuned models introduce system prompts and RLHF-induced stylistic shifts that confound depth estimation.\n(3)~\\emph{Threshold}: $\\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$; 7/9 continuation-mode configurations yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Gemma-2-2B, Mistral-7B: $\\Kempirical{=}1$); a $\\theta$-free changepoint analysis agrees within $\\pm 1$ for 35/37 configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n(4)~\\emph{Marginal boundary}: Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\\theta$ by only 0.002; cross-scorer validation drops this to $\\Kempirical{=}1$ (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}). LLaMA-3.1-8B (C4) and Mistral-7B-v0.3 (code) represent the most marginal $\\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, with 1v2 CI lower bounds within 0.3 percentage points of $\\theta$.\n(5)~\\emph{Feature set}: the 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher); $K^*_{\\text{neural}}$ is model-dependent ($3$ for Qwen, $2$ for Pythia/OLMo).\n(6)~\\emph{Segment length}: $\\Kempirical{=}2$ confirmed for 128 and 256 tokens (CI lower $> \\theta$); 512 tokens yields point estimate $\\Kempirical{=}2$ but CI lower marginally below $\\theta$ ($0.5979$); the boundary weakens at longer generation lengths.\n(7)~\\emph{Curation applicability}: the filtering evaluation is validated on two architectures (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct and Pythia-1.4B) with consistent strategy ordering (Appendix~\\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}), though absolute improvement remains small and effect sizes diminish at lower contamination ratios (10--50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).\n(8)~\\emph{Family balance}: the Qwen2.5 family provides 5 of 10 total configurations (4 of 9 continuation-mode); though spanning three scales (1.5B, 7B, 14B) and two training variants (base, instruct), aggregate statistics weight this family more heavily than the five single-configuration families.\nThe pipeline scales linearly with corpus size; the computational bottleneck is scorer inference, which can be parallelized across documents.\n(9)~\\emph{Transfer coverage}: cross-model transfer validation shows 80--92\\% accuracy retention at $\\leq$1.5B (4 configurations) and 96--100\\% mean pairwise AUC retention at 7B (3 models, 3 architecture families; Appendix~\\ref{sec:7b_transfer}); however, at the $\\Kempirical$ boundary (1v2), 4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$, indicating that boundary-level transfer remains fragile even as aggregate transfer strengthens with scale.\n(10)~\\emph{Mixed chains}: estimating $\\Kempirical$ under interleaved prompt-chain and retrain-chain contamination (the dominant real-world scenario) requires chain-type annotation not available in web corpora; our $[2,7]$ bounds may not compose additively.\n(11)~\\emph{Feature ceiling}: larger or domain-adapted encoder scorers may tighten the $\\Kempirical$ upper bound; our 15D measurement uses a fixed Pythia-1.4B scorer, and DeBERTa-large extends $\\Kempirical$ by $+1$ only on structured domains, suggesting headroom remains.\nAdditional limitations (cross-model scoring sensitivity, feature ranking variability at the 1v2 boundary, structural composition confound analysis) appear in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.\n\n% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nGenerational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing, positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.\nOur experiments use publicly available corpora (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv abstracts) and do not involve personally identifiable information.\nA potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\\auc \\geq 0.96$ at 0v1) remains robust.\nCode and generated corpora will be released upon acceptance to enable reproducibility.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\input{appendix}\n\\input{appendix_extended}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

113:\newlabel{fig:pipeline}{{1}{2}{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\delta $ to produce a depth chain. Statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction}{figure.caption.1}{}}
419:\newlabel{fig:kstar_theta}{{2}{5}{(a) Pairwise $\auc $ decay across depth pairs for model-mode configurations (solid) and decoding strategies (dashed); horizontal dashed line marks $\theta = 0.60$; AUC computed on held-out folds. (b) $\Kempirical (\theta )$ sensitivity: all configurations yield $\Kempirical {=}2$ at $\theta {=}0.60$; at $\theta {=}0.70$, all drop to $\Kempirical {=}1$}{figure.caption.11}{}}
464:\newlabel{fig:transfer_heatmap}{{3}{5}{Cross-model transfer heatmap. Each cell shows 6-class ordinal accuracy when trained on the row model and tested on the column model, divided by in-domain accuracy (retention rate). Pythia$\leftrightarrow $OLMo: 90--92\%; Qwen-rewrite$\to $continuation: 61--63\%}{figure.caption.13}{}}

1153:\newlabel{fig:filtering_strategies}{{5}{12}{Downstream performance of all filtering strategies (5 seeds, $\pm $1 std).
